UICOPILOT decouples the generation process into two stages: (1) generating a coarse-grained HTML hierarchical structure (DOM tree) using a Vision Transformer (ViT)-based model and bounding box pruning, and (2) generating fine-grained HTML/CSS code for leaf nodes and refining non-leaf node styles using GPT-4V. The experimental results on the WebCode2M dataset demonstrate that UICOPILOT outperforms existing baselines in both automatic and human evaluations, particularly in handling complex webpages. The authors perform ablation studies to validate the design choices and present a case study to highlight the advantages of their approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The core novelty of this paper lies in the hierarchical, two-stage approach to UI code generation. While the individual components (ViT-based structure prediction, LLM-based code generation) are not entirely novel, their combination in the proposed architecture to specifically address the challenges of lengthy code and complex nested structures in webpage synthesis represents a significant improvement over the existing one-step generation methods. BBox pruning and the specific prompt engineering used with GPT-4V contribute incrementally to the performance. The approach of using BBoxes as anchors for subregion image cropping and subsequent local code generation is well thought out.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses a practically significant problem: reducing the development time and effort for front-end UI creation. The reported improvements over existing baselines, especially the human evaluation results, indicate that UICOPILOT is a promising solution for automating UI synthesis. The ability to handle complex, real-world webpages is a key strength. By decoupling the DOM structure prediction from detailed code generation, this work provides a valuable direction for future research.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-defined Problem:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of existing one-step approaches to UI code generation.
*   **Novel Approach:** The hierarchical code generation architecture effectively addresses the identified challenges.
*   **Solid Experimental Results:** The experiments on a real-world dataset (WebCode2M) demonstrate the effectiveness of UICOPILOT against strong baselines.
*   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of different components of the proposed architecture.
*   **Human Evaluation:**  Human evaluations provide crucial, subjective validation of the generated UI.
*   **Good Writing Quality:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependency on GPT-4V:** The reliance on a proprietary LLM (GPT-4V) is a potential limitation for reproducibility and accessibility.  The paper doesn't thoroughly discuss strategies to mitigate this dependency, such as exploring alternatives or fine-tuning open-source models.
*   **Limited discussion on failure cases:** A more detailed analysis of the types of errors UICOPILOT makes, and a comparison with the types of errors GPT-4V generates would have provided additional insight. What are the intrinsic limitations of a hierarchical approach?
*   **BBox Pruning Details:** The description of the BBox pruning heuristic could be more thorough. Are the thresholds set empirically? How sensitive is the overall performance to these parameters?
*   **Justification of Design Choices:**  While the authors perform experiments and explain the approach well, there are a few design aspects lacking a stronger justification.  For example, why choose Pix2Struct over other transformer-based vision models? Could more recent vision models further improve performance?
*   **Lack of comparison with very recent methods.** The field is moving fast, and some very recent code generation from images methods could have also been included.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to significantly influence research in UI automation, code generation, and multimodal learning. The hierarchical approach is a generalizable concept that could be applied to other code generation tasks. The insights from the ablation studies and the human evaluations will be valuable for researchers working on improving UI code generation models.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to UI code generation with compelling experimental results. The hierarchical architecture and the integration of a ViT-based model with GPT-4V are significant contributions. The human evaluations provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of UICOPILOT. However, the reliance on a proprietary LLM and the lack of thorough analysis of failure cases, along with limited discussion of design choices and comparison with other very recent methods, slightly detract from its overall impact. Nevertheless, the paper is a valuable contribution to the field and has the potential to inspire future research.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### PIG: Privacy Jailbreak Attack on LLMs via Gradient-based Iterative In-Context Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09921v1)
- **Authors**: Yidan Wang, Yanan Cao, Yubing Ren, Fang Fang, Zheng Lin, Binxing Fang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) excel in various domains but pose inherent privacy risks. Existing methods to evaluate privacy leakage in LLMs often use memorized prefixes or simple instructions to extract data, both of which well-alignment models can easily block. Meanwhile, Jailbreak attacks bypass LLM safety mechanisms to generate harmful content, but their role in privacy scenarios remains underexplored. In this paper, we examine the effectiveness of jailbreak attacks in extracting sensitive information, bridging privacy leakage and jailbreak attacks in LLMs. Moreover, we propose PIG, a novel framework targeting Personally Identifiable Information (PII) and addressing the limitations of current jailbreak methods. Specifically, PIG identifies PII entities and their types in privacy queries, uses in-context learning to build a privacy context, and iteratively updates it with three gradient-based strategies to elicit target PII. We evaluate PIG and existing jailbreak methods using two privacy-related datasets. Experiments on four white-box and two black-box LLMs show that PIG outperforms baseline methods and achieves state-of-the-art (SoTA) results. The results underscore significant privacy risks in LLMs, emphasizing the need for stronger safeguards. Our code is availble at \href{https://github.com/redwyd/PrivacyJailbreak}{https://github.com/redwyd/PrivacyJailbreak}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "PIG: Privacy Jailbreak Attack on LLMs via Gradient-based Iterative In-Context Optimization" investigates the vulnerability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to privacy jailbreak attacks.  It bridges the gap between standard jailbreak attacks, typically aimed at generating harmful content, and privacy leakage scenarios. The core contribution is a novel framework called PIG, designed to extract Personally Identifiable Information (PII) from LLMs. PIG identifies PII entities and types in queries, uses in-context learning to build a privacy context, and then iteratively refines this context using three gradient-based optimization strategies to elicit the target PII. The authors evaluate PIG against several baseline jailbreak methods on two privacy-related datasets, demonstrating superior performance on both white-box and black-box LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The idea of adapting jailbreak attacks for privacy scenarios is not entirely new, but PIG introduces several novel elements. The combined approach of PII-aware in-context learning with gradient-based iterative optimization of the privacy context is a key innovation. Existing jailbreak methods often lack focus when applied to privacy, and traditional privacy attacks are becoming less effective due to improved LLM alignment. PIG attempts to address both limitations. The use of different gradient-based strategies, and especially their combination, also demonstrates novelty.  The combination of random, entity-focused, and dynamic token replacement strategies for optimizing in-context examples contributes to the innovation.

* **Significance:** Privacy leakage in LLMs is a serious concern, and this paper contributes to a deeper understanding of this problem. By showing how jailbreak techniques can be adapted and significantly improved to extract PII, the authors highlight the vulnerabilities of even well-aligned models. Demonstrating PIG's state-of-the-art (SoTA) performance suggests that existing safeguards are insufficient. The benchmark analysis of existing jailbreak attacks in the privacy context is also valuable for the research community.  The findings underscore the need for more robust privacy defenses.

* **Strengths:**
    *   The PIG framework is well-defined and technically sound.
    *   The experimental evaluation is thorough, covering several white-box and black-box models and datasets.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component of PIG.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the methodology and results.
    *   The analysis of successful attacks and failure cases in the appendix strengthens the paper.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   The ethical warning at the start feels a little misplaced. All jailbreak attacks could be considered to generate potentially offensive/harmful content and do not need explicit mention.
    *   The threat model assumes full access to open-source models in the white-box setting. While the paper shows transferability to black-box models, the assumption might limit the real-world applicability.
    *  The paper mentions retrieval of PII from online databases, this seems to go against the point of not leaking any PII from the demonstrations themselves, but it's not detailed enough to fully confirm that.
    *   The reliance on GPT-4 for PII identification might introduce bias, as GPT-4 is itself an LLM with its own limitations.  While convenient, this aspect reduces the paper's self-containedness.
    *   While showing superiority to other jailbreak methods for this particular task, a deeper analysis regarding the transferability and robustness of the crafted in-context examples to other queries or LLMs would enhance the paper.  Would a slightly changed query render the optimized context useless?

* **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence research on privacy-preserving LLMs and the development of more robust defense mechanisms.  It provides a strong baseline for future work in this area.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and technically sound framework for privacy jailbreak attacks on LLMs. The experimental results demonstrate PIG's effectiveness, and the ablation studies provide valuable insights.  The work fills a gap in the literature by specifically focusing on privacy aspects of jailbreak attacks, offering a nuanced perspective beyond simply generating harmful content.  However, the dependence on GPT-4 for PII identification, the assumption of full access in the white-box setting, and the general limitations inherent in any jailbreak-focused study (e.g., the "arms race" between attacks and defenses) temper the impact slightly. While the black-box experiments demonstrate potential real-world applicability, more robustness analysis of the generated prompts would further bolster this aspect. The paper provides a significant step forward in understanding privacy vulnerabilities in LLMs, warranting a score of 7.5, indicating a strong and noteworthy contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Improving the Euclidean Diffusion Generation of Manifold Data by Mitigating Score Function Singularity
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09922v1)
- **Authors**: Zichen Liu, Wei Zhang, Tiejun Li
- **Abstract**: Euclidean diffusion models have achieved remarkable success in generative modeling across diverse domains, and they have been extended to manifold case in recent advances. Instead of explicitly utilizing the structure of special manifolds as studied in previous works, we investigate direct sampling of the Euclidean diffusion models for general manifold-constrained data in this paper. We reveal the multiscale singularity of the score function in the embedded space of manifold, which hinders the accuracy of diffusion-generated samples. We then present an elaborate theoretical analysis of the singularity structure of the score function by separating it along the tangential and normal directions of the manifold. To mitigate the singularity and improve the sampling accuracy, we propose two novel methods: (1) Niso-DM, which introduces non-isotropic noise along the normal direction to reduce scale discrepancies, and (2) Tango-DM, which trains only the tangential component of the score function using a tangential-only loss function. Numerical experiments demonstrate that our methods achieve superior performance on distributions over various manifolds with complex geometries.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the provided paper. Here's a summary, followed by a critical evaluation of its novelty, significance, and an assigned score:

**Paper Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of generating data constrained to manifolds using Euclidean diffusion models. It identifies a key challenge: the multiscale singularity of the score function when applying Euclidean diffusion models directly to manifold data.  The paper demonstrates that this singularity is particularly pronounced along the normal directions of the manifold. To mitigate this issue, the authors propose two novel methods: (1) **Niso-DM**, which introduces non-isotropic noise, adding more noise along the normal direction to reduce scale discrepancy; and (2) **Tango-DM**, which trains only the tangential component of the score function, avoiding the learning difficulties caused by the multiscale normal component. The paper provides theoretical analysis of the score function singularity and the effect of the proposed methods, along with experimental results demonstrating improved performance on various manifolds compared to existing Euclidean diffusion models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Score Function Singularity Analysis:** The paper's detailed theoretical analysis of the score function's multiscale singularity, particularly its separation into tangential and normal components, is a significant contribution. While previous works have noted the singularity issue, this paper provides a more refined and insightful characterization. Further, the connection between the ambient space score function and the Riemannian score function is a novel aspect that previous works did not address.
*   **Niso-DM:** The idea of using non-isotropic noise is novel and well-motivated by the theoretical analysis. It provides a way to balance the influence of the tangential and normal components during the diffusion process.
*   **Tango-DM:** Training only the tangential component of the score function is a potentially impactful idea. It directly addresses the source of instability in the training process by circumventing the need to approximate the singular normal component.

**Significance:**

*   **Practical Applicability:**  The paper's focus on directly applying Euclidean diffusion models to manifold data, rather than relying on specialized Riemannian diffusion models, is highly relevant. Euclidean diffusion models are well-established and readily available, making the proposed methods practically attractive.
*   **Improved Performance:** The experimental results demonstrate that the proposed methods (Niso-DM and Tango-DM) achieve superior performance on various manifolds with complex geometries. This is crucial for applying generative models in fields such as molecular modeling, robotics, and computer graphics.
*   **Theoretical Foundation:** The theoretical analysis provides a solid foundation for the proposed methods, contributing to a deeper understanding of diffusion models on manifolds. This theoretical understanding guides the development of practical solutions.

**Strengths:**

*   Strong theoretical analysis of the singularity problem.
*   Two well-motivated and novel methods (Niso-DM and Tango-DM).
*   Comprehensive experimental evaluation on diverse manifolds.
*   Clear and well-written presentation.

**Weaknesses:**

*   The theoretical analysis makes several assumptions (e.g., smoothness of the manifold, uniqueness of the closest point on the manifold), which might not hold in all real-world scenarios.
*   The performance gains, while significant, could be more substantial. It would be good to see more significant improvements over state-of-the-art on larger-scale, more complex manifold datasets.
*   The hyperparameter sensitivity of Cniso and Ctango needs further investigation to establish how to set these without extensive tuning in different application scenarios.
*   While the paper claims to address manifold overfitting, this aspect could benefit from more explicit investigation and comparison with alternative techniques to prevent overfitting.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of generative modeling on manifolds. The refined theoretical analysis, combined with the two novel methods (Niso-DM and Tango-DM), addresses a significant challenge in a practical and well-motivated way. The paper provides a solid theoretical foundation and demonstrates improved performance through comprehensive experiments. While the paper has some limitations, as outlined above, the strengths far outweigh the weaknesses. The work represents a significant advance in understanding and applying Euclidean diffusion models to manifold-constrained data. It is a solid, rigorous paper that will likely influence future research in this area.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### From Trade-off to Synergy: A Versatile Symbiotic Watermarking Framework for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09924v1)
- **Authors**: Yidan Wang, Yubing Ren, Yanan Cao, Binxing Fang
- **Abstract**: The rise of Large Language Models (LLMs) has heightened concerns about the misuse of AI-generated text, making watermarking a promising solution. Mainstream watermarking schemes for LLMs fall into two categories: logits-based and sampling-based. However, current schemes entail trade-offs among robustness, text quality, and security. To mitigate this, we integrate logits-based and sampling-based schemes, harnessing their respective strengths to achieve synergy. In this paper, we propose a versatile symbiotic watermarking framework with three strategies: serial, parallel, and hybrid. The hybrid framework adaptively embeds watermarks using token entropy and semantic entropy, optimizing the balance between detectability, robustness, text quality, and security. Furthermore, we validate our approach through comprehensive experiments on various datasets and models. Experimental results indicate that our method outperforms existing baselines and achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance. We believe this framework provides novel insights into diverse watermarking paradigms. Our code is available at \href{https://github.com/redwyd/SymMark}{https://github.com/redwyd/SymMark}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SymMark, a versatile symbiotic watermarking framework for large language models (LLMs). This framework integrates logits-based and sampling-based watermarking techniques to overcome the limitations of each approach individually. It presents three strategies: Serial, Parallel, and Hybrid symbiotic watermarking. The hybrid strategy, which is the primary configuration, adaptively embeds watermarks based on token and semantic entropy, aiming to balance detectability, robustness, text quality, and security. The authors provide a unified detection algorithm and conduct extensive experiments across multiple datasets and models, demonstrating that SymMark outperforms existing baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The core idea of combining logits-based and sampling-based watermarking is itself not entirely novel, but the paper differentiates itself through its systematic approach to integrating these techniques and its adaptive hybrid strategy.  Existing works have explored hybrid approaches (though not necessarily using these exact terms), but SymMark appears to be more deliberate and comprehensive in its exploration of different integration strategies and adaptive selection based on entropy.  The unified detection algorithm contributes to the overall completeness of the framework.

**Significance:**

The problem of watermarking LLMs is significant due to the risks of misuse, copyright infringement, and disinformation.  SymMark tackles a key challenge in watermarking: balancing robustness, text quality, and security. The experimental results, showing state-of-the-art performance in certain aspects, suggests that the framework offers a promising approach. However, the security analysis is somewhat limited. Showing resistance to frequency attacks is good, but ideally, a more rigorous formal security argument or evaluation against a wider range of adaptive attacks would be expected to really claim it's secure.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Approach:** The paper systematically explores different integration strategies (Serial, Parallel, Hybrid) and offers a unified detection algorithm.
*   **Adaptive Hybrid Strategy:** The adaptive selection of watermarking methods based on entropy is a strong point, potentially allowing for more nuanced and effective watermarking.
*   **Extensive Evaluation:** The authors conduct a thorough set of experiments across various datasets, models, and attack scenarios.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and organized, making it relatively easy to understand the proposed framework and experimental results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Security Analysis:** While the paper addresses watermark stealing attacks, a more rigorous formal security analysis or evaluation against a wider range of adaptive attacks is needed. The existing attack is relatively basic.
*   **Entropy as the Sole Adaptation Metric:** Relying solely on token and semantic entropy may not be sufficient. Other factors, such as contextual information or semantic relationships between tokens, could be considered for more informed adaptation.
*   **Incremental Improvement:** While the results demonstrate improvements over baselines, the improvements are perhaps incremental rather than revolutionary. The reported numbers don't always tell the full story and should be inspected closely to understand *where* the gains are happening and *why*.
*  **Lack of ablation study**: No ablation study to show the contribution of different components of the "Hybrid" approach, e.g., what part of it is because of using both techniques versus the adaptation part.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper could influence future research on LLM watermarking by:

*   Encouraging the exploration of hybrid watermarking techniques that combine the strengths of different approaches.
*   Highlighting the importance of adaptive watermarking strategies that consider token context and semantic information.
*   Providing a framework for evaluating the trade-offs between robustness, text quality, and security in watermarking schemes.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a well-engineered and empirically validated watermarking framework for LLMs. However, the novelty is somewhat limited, and the security analysis lacks depth. While the experimental results are promising, the improvements are incremental. The dependence on entropy as the key adaptation metric may also be a limiting factor.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Reinforced Interactive Continual Learning via Real-time Noisy Human Feedback
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09925v1)
- **Authors**: Yutao Yang, Jie Zhou, Junsong Li, Qianjun Pan, Bihao Zhan, Qin Chen, Xipeng Qiu, Liang He
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces an interactive continual learning paradigm where AI models dynamically learn new skills from real-time human feedback while retaining prior knowledge. This paradigm distinctively addresses two major limitations of traditional continual learning: (1) dynamic model updates using streaming, real-time human-annotated data, rather than static datasets with fixed labels, and (2) the assumption of clean labels, by explicitly handling the noisy feedback common in real-world interactions. To tackle these problems, we propose RiCL, a Reinforced interactive Continual Learning framework leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) to learn new skills effectively from dynamic feedback. RiCL incorporates three key components: a temporal consistency-aware purifier to automatically discern clean from noisy samples in data streams; an interaction-aware direct preference optimization strategy to align model behavior with human intent by reconciling AI-generated and human-provided feedback; and a noise-resistant contrastive learning module that captures robust representations by exploiting inherent data relationships, thus avoiding reliance on potentially unreliable labels. Extensive experiments on two benchmark datasets (FewRel and TACRED), contaminated with realistic noise patterns, demonstrate that our RiCL approach substantially outperforms existing combinations of state-of-the-art online continual learning and noisy-label learning methods.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary of the paper, followed by a rigorous and critical evaluation of its novelty and significance, along with a justified score.

**Summary:**

The paper "Reinforced Interactive Continual Learning via Real-time Noisy Human Feedback" (RiCL) proposes a new continual learning (CL) paradigm that addresses two key limitations of traditional CL: (1) the reliance on static datasets with fixed labels, and (2) the assumption of clean labels. The RiCL framework tackles these issues by enabling AI models to dynamically learn from streaming, real-time human feedback, while simultaneously handling the noisy nature of that feedback.  RiCL leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) and incorporates three main components: a temporal consistency-aware purifier (TCP) to distinguish between clean and noisy data, an interaction-aware direct preference optimization (IPO) strategy to align model behavior with human intent, and a noise-resistant contrastive learning module (NCL) to learn robust representations. The authors conduct experiments on the FewRel and Tacred datasets, demonstrating that RiCL outperforms existing online CL and noisy-label learning methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   The paper's novelty lies primarily in its integration of several existing techniques within a novel and practically relevant **interactive continual learning paradigm**. While the individual components (TCP, IPO, NCL) are not entirely novel, their synergistic combination within a framework that explicitly addresses real-time noisy human feedback is a significant contribution.
    *   The **application of direct preference optimization** within a continual learning context that also explicitly addresses noisy feedback represents a key novel element. This approach enables the model to more effectively learn from imperfect human input.
    *   The temporal consistency-aware purifier (TCP) is a reasonable approach, building on existing ideas of confidence and prediction stability.  However, it is somewhat incremental and could be seen as an adaptation of existing sample selection techniques.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The RiCL framework is highly significant due to its potential to improve the practicality and applicability of continual learning in real-world interactive AI systems. The paper directly addresses a crucial gap in the existing literature by acknowledging and handling noisy human feedback, which is an inherent characteristic of human-machine interaction.
    *   The demonstrated improvements over existing methods, particularly the combination of online continual learning and noisy label learning, provide strong empirical evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    *   By showing robust performance on both FewRel and Tacred datasets the authors suggest the framework may be transferable to a variety of tasks and domains.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper clearly articulates the problem and provides a well-defined framework to address it.
    *   The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, with comparisons to a wide range of strong baseline methods.
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contributions of each component of the RiCL framework.
    *   The discussion of task order influence provides further support for the robustness of RiCL.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The simulated noise may not fully capture the complexities and nuances of real-world human feedback. More realistic noise models or real human subject experiments may be more helpful.
    *   The selection of FewRel and Tacred, while standard datasets, may limit the generalizability of the results. Evaluation on larger, more diverse datasets would be beneficial.
    *   While the authors mention limitations of the method relating to symmetric noise and single-turn feedback, they do not conduct experiments to explore their effects.

*   **Potential Impact:**

    *   This paper has the potential to significantly influence the development of more practical and robust continual learning systems.
    *   The proposed framework provides a solid foundation for future research on interactive continual learning, encouraging the exploration of more sophisticated noise handling techniques and human-machine interaction models.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper is a strong contribution to the field of continual learning. It presents a novel and well-designed framework that addresses a critical gap in the literature by handling real-time noisy human feedback. The experimental results provide strong empirical evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed approach. While the simulations and datasets do have some limitations, they do not detract significantly from the overall value and potential impact of this research.
Taking all the above points into consideration, I believe a score of **7** is appropriate. The RiCL framework is novel in its design and its incorporation of direct preference optimization and demonstrates the potential of interactive continual learning. The paper is well-written and supported by empirical evidence, but limitations in the experimental setup and limited real-world evaluation prevent it from receiving a higher score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Rethinking Prompt Optimizers: From Prompt Merits to Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09930v1)
- **Authors**: Zixiao Zhu, Hanzhang Zhou, Zijian Feng, Tianjiao Li, Chua Jia Jim Deryl, Mak Lee Onn, Gee Wah Ng, Kezhi Mao
- **Abstract**: Prompt optimization (PO) offers a practical alternative to fine-tuning large language models (LLMs), enabling performance improvements without altering model weights. Existing methods typically rely on advanced, large-scale LLMs like GPT-4 to generate optimized prompts. However, due to limited downward compatibility, verbose, instruction-heavy prompts from advanced LLMs can overwhelm lightweight inference models and degrade response quality. In this work, we rethink prompt optimization through the lens of interpretable design. We first identify a set of model-agnostic prompt quality merits and empirically validate their effectiveness in enhancing prompt and response quality. We then introduce MePO, a merit-guided, lightweight, and locally deployable prompt optimizer trained on our preference dataset built from merit-aligned prompts generated by a lightweight LLM. Unlike prior work, MePO avoids online optimization reliance, reduces cost and privacy concerns, and, by learning clear, interpretable merits, generalizes effectively to both large-scale and lightweight inference models. Experiments demonstrate that MePO achieves better results across diverse tasks and model types, offering a scalable and robust solution for real-world deployment. Our model and dataset are available at: https://github.com/MidiyaZhu/MePO
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Rethinking Prompt Optimizers: From Prompt Merits to Optimization" challenges the prevalent reliance on large, advanced language models (LLMs) like GPT-4 for prompt optimization (PO). It argues that overly verbose, instruction-heavy prompts generated by these LLMs can overwhelm smaller inference models, degrading performance. The authors propose a novel approach called MePO (Merit-Guided Prompt Optimization), which focuses on interpretable prompt design. First, they identify and validate model-agnostic prompt quality merits. Then, they create a preference dataset of prompts optimized by a lightweight LLM, guided by these merits. Finally, they train MePO on this dataset. MePO avoids online optimization, reduces costs, addresses privacy concerns, and generalizes effectively across both large and small models. Experiments demonstrate MePO's improved performance across various tasks and model types.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its shift away from simply leveraging the scale of advanced LLMs for prompt optimization to a more interpretable and model-agnostic approach. The idea of explicitly identifying, formalizing, and learning "prompt merits" is a key contribution.  While previous work has considered prompt optimization, this merit-based, lightweight LLM-focused approach represents a significant departure from the prevailing paradigm.  Furthermore, the systematic empirical analysis to identify these merits adds to the paper's contribution.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper is substantial.  By demonstrating that lightweight LLMs can be effective prompt optimizers when guided by well-defined merits, the authors offer a more practical and scalable solution for real-world deployment.  This is particularly relevant in resource-constrained environments or where privacy is a concern. The improved downward compatibility (where prompts optimized for larger models still work well on smaller ones) is also crucial for practical applicability. The findings have the potential to influence how prompts are designed and optimized, particularly for applications using smaller models or in privacy-sensitive scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined approach:** The paper provides a clear and well-defined methodology, from merit identification to dataset construction and model training.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The experiments are comprehensive, covering a variety of tasks, models, and baseline comparisons. The ablation study demonstrating the value of the training step and the demonstration of upward and downward compatibility is especially compelling.
    *   **Empirical grounding:**  The approach is grounded in empirical analysis, demonstrating a deep understanding of prompt optimization behavior.
    *   **Practical implications:**  The paper has practical implications for deploying LLMs in real-world scenarios, addressing cost, privacy, and scalability issues.
    *   **Clear Writing and Presentation:** The paper is well written and well-structured, making it easy to follow the authors’ argument and understand their methodology.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of Merits:** While the identified merits are well-justified, there might be other relevant prompt quality dimensions that were not considered. Further research could explore a broader set of merits and their interdependencies.
    *   **Dataset Dependency:** The performance of MePO is inherently tied to the quality and diversity of the prompt preference dataset. While the dataset is carefully constructed, it's possible that its specific characteristics may limit the generalizability of the learned merits.
    *   **Potential for Incremental Gain:** While the merit-based approach is novel, one could argue that it might still be considered an incremental improvement over simply fine-tuning a smaller LLM directly on task-specific data.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of prompt engineering and LLM deployment.  It encourages a shift toward interpretable and model-agnostic prompt design, which could lead to more robust and scalable solutions.  The emphasis on lightweight LLMs as prompt optimizers could also democratize access to prompt engineering tools, making them more accessible to researchers and practitioners with limited resources.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of prompt optimization. While there are some limitations, the strengths of the approach, particularly its practicality and improved downward compatibility, outweigh these weaknesses. The paper challenges existing assumptions, provides a well-defined methodology, and offers a promising direction for future research. The score is slightly below "exceptional" as the scope is relatively limited and future work is required to assess the merits' generality and long-term practical impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### CartoAgent: a multimodal large language model-powered multi-agent cartographic framework for map style transfer and evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09936v1)
- **Authors**: Chenglong Wang, Yuhao Kang, Zhaoya Gong, Pengjun Zhao, Yu Feng, Wenjia Zhang, Ge Li
- **Abstract**: The rapid development of generative artificial intelligence (GenAI) presents new opportunities to advance the cartographic process. Previous studies have either overlooked the artistic aspects of maps or faced challenges in creating both accurate and informative maps. In this study, we propose CartoAgent, a novel multi-agent cartographic framework powered by multimodal large language models (MLLMs). This framework simulates three key stages in cartographic practice: preparation, map design, and evaluation. At each stage, different MLLMs act as agents with distinct roles to collaborate, discuss, and utilize tools for specific purposes. In particular, CartoAgent leverages MLLMs' visual aesthetic capability and world knowledge to generate maps that are both visually appealing and informative. By separating style from geographic data, it can focus on designing stylesheets without modifying the vector-based data, thereby ensuring geographic accuracy. We applied CartoAgent to a specific task centered on map restyling-namely, map style transfer and evaluation. The effectiveness of this framework was validated through extensive experiments and a human evaluation study. CartoAgent can be extended to support a variety of cartographic design decisions and inform future integrations of GenAI in cartography.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CartoAgent, a novel multi-agent cartographic framework powered by multimodal large language models (MLLMs) for map style transfer and evaluation. The framework simulates three key stages in cartographic practice: preparation, map design, and evaluation. Different MLLMs act as agents with distinct roles to collaborate, discuss, and utilize tools for specific purposes. CartoAgent leverages MLLMs' visual aesthetic capability and world knowledge to generate visually appealing and informative maps. By separating style from geographic data, it focuses on designing stylesheets without modifying the vector-based data, ensuring geographic accuracy. The framework is applied to map style transfer and evaluation, validated through experiments and human evaluation. The paper argues that CartoAgent can be extended to support a variety of cartographic design decisions and inform future integrations of GenAI in cartography.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its application of a multi-agent MLLM framework to the specific problem of map style transfer and evaluation. While MLLMs and multi-agent systems are not entirely new concepts, their combination in the cartographic domain, with a focus on separating style from data, represents a genuine contribution. The paper moves beyond simple map understanding tasks to map creation and aesthetic evaluation, which is a notable advance.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its potential to democratize map design and enhance the automation of cartographic processes. The framework addresses limitations of existing AI-based map generation approaches, particularly the lack of accuracy and informativeness often found in raster-based methods. The human evaluation study provides evidence that the MLLM's evaluation criteria align reasonably well with human judgment, suggesting that CartoAgent can automate aspects of map critique effectively. The separation of style from geographic data maintains accuracy.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies gaps in the existing literature concerning the use of GenAI for cartography and formulates a focused research question.
    *   **Well-Defined Framework:** The CartoAgent framework is well-structured, with clear definitions of agent roles, interactions, and capabilities.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes multi-scale and multi-source map style transfer experiments and a human evaluation study, providing strong evidence for the framework's effectiveness.
    *   **Emphasis on Cartographic Principles:**  The design incorporates key cartographic principles (e.g., separating style from data, considering semantic information) leading to more effective maps.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Specific Models:** The paper heavily relies on GPT-4o and DALL·E. While these are powerful models, the framework's generalizability to other MLLMs is not thoroughly explored.
    *   **Implementation Details:** While the prompt design is detailed, certain implementation aspects (e.g., specific configurations of the Mapbox platform) might limit reproducibility.
    *   **Limited Scope:**  The application is limited to map style transfer and evaluation. The framework's adaptability to broader cartographic design tasks remains to be more fully demonstrated in the paper.
    *   **Ethical Concerns:** Although the ethical issues are briefly discussed, a more in-depth analysis of copyright, data privacy, and potential biases in the MLLMs would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Computational Cost and Scalability:** The paper doesn't address the computational costs associated with MLLMs and their impact on the framework's scalability.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in automated cartography, particularly the development of AI-assisted design tools and the integration of MLLMs in map creation workflows. The emphasis on accuracy, informativeness, and visual appeal could shape the development of more user-friendly and effective mapping solutions.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to map style transfer and evaluation using MLLMs and a multi-agent framework. The results are promising, demonstrating that CartoAgent can generate visually appealing and informative maps while preserving geographic accuracy. The human evaluation study provides further support for the framework's effectiveness. However, the paper has certain limitations in terms of generalizability, implementation details, scope, and ethical considerations. Although the ethical issues are briefly discussed, a more in-depth analysis of copyright, data privacy, and potential biases in the MLLMs would strengthen the paper. Also, the paper doesn't address the computational costs associated with MLLMs and their impact on the framework's scalability. Considering these strengths and weaknesses, a score of 7 reflects the paper's significant contribution to the field while acknowledging areas for future improvement.

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Design and Evaluation of Generative Agent-based Platform for Human-Assistant Interaction Research: A Tale of 10 User Studies
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09938v1)
- **Authors**: Ziyi Xuan, Yiwen Wu, Xuhai Xu, Vinod Namboodiri, Mooi Choo Chuah, Yu Yang
- **Abstract**: Designing and evaluating personalized and proactive assistant agents remains challenging due to the time, cost, and ethical concerns associated with human-in-the-loop experimentation. Existing Human-Computer Interaction (HCI) methods often require extensive physical setup and human participation, which introduces privacy concerns and limits scalability. Simulated environments offer a partial solution but are typically constrained by rule-based scenarios and still depend heavily on human input to guide interactions and interpret results. Recent advances in large language models (LLMs) have introduced the possibility of generative agents that can simulate realistic human behavior, reasoning, and social dynamics. However, their effectiveness in modeling human-assistant interactions remains largely unexplored. To address this gap, we present a generative agent-based simulation platform designed to simulate human-assistant interactions. We identify ten prior studies on assistant agents that span different aspects of interaction design and replicate these studies using our simulation platform. Our results show that fully simulated experiments using generative agents can approximate key aspects of human-assistant interactions. Based on these simulations, we are able to replicate the core conclusions of the original studies. Our work provides a scalable and cost-effective approach for studying assistant agent design without requiring live human subjects. We will open source both the platform and collected results from the experiments on our website: https://dash-gidea.github.io/.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper, including a novelty/significance score.

**Summary:**

The paper presents GIDEA, a generative agent-based simulation platform designed for human-assistant interaction research. The core premise is to use LLMs to simulate human behavior in assistant interaction scenarios, replacing or augmenting costly and ethically complex human-in-the-loop experiments. The authors identify key challenges in this area (data limitations, variations in research setups, limited behavioral data) and propose a modular platform design that allows for configurable personas, environments, interaction protocols, and traceable workflows. They replicate ten previously published human-assistant interaction studies using GIDEA, showing that the platform can reproduce key behavioral patterns observed in human-subject studies. The results validate the fidelity and generalizability of generative agent-based behavioral simulation in controlled study settings, providing a cost-effective and scalable alternative for exploring assistant agent designs before involving human participants. The authors promise to open-source the platform and collected results.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a Significant Problem:** The paper tackles a critical bottleneck in human-assistant interaction research: the difficulty and cost of running large-scale, longitudinal human studies.
*   **Novel Approach:**  Using LLMs to simulate human behavior in this domain is a promising avenue and relatively novel. While simulated environments exist, the use of *generative* agents to produce contextually rich and adaptable behavior significantly advances the field.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** Replicating ten diverse studies is a strong validation strategy. The authors didn't simply reproduce the results, but critically analyzed how the simulated outcomes aligned with the original findings and identified variations based on study design and spontaneous events.
*   **Well-Structured Platform:** The platform design is well-defined and modular, addressing the challenges of diverse research setups. The focus on traceability is crucial for evaluating and improving simulation fidelity.
*   **Reproducibility & Open Source:** The commitment to open-sourcing both the platform and the collected data is commendable. This allows the research community to build upon this work and validate its findings.
*   **Clarity and Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly structured, making it easy to understand the core concepts and contributions.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Simulation Fidelity:** While the paper shows that the LLM-driven experiments can replicate the *key aspects* of interactions, questions still arise about its ability to fully replicate human behavior, especially regarding emotional states, cognitive load, and social nuances. The authors acknowledge this limitation by noting that the avatar simulations were more likely to respond in various scenarios than real users. The paper does not fully address the potential for LLMs to simply be "parroting" back patterns they have been trained on, rather than demonstrating genuine understanding or complex social reasoning.
*   **Limited Dataset Access:** The authors acknowledge that complete logs are not released in prior HCI studies. As a result, it is difficult to verify how close the simulation results resemble the results of the actual studies.
*   **Reliance on GPT-4:** The current implementation relies on a closed-source LLM (GPT-4), which could hinder reproducibility and limit modifications and customization in the future if GPT-4 access changes. An open-source alternative would have provided a wider community.
*   **Generalizability Concerns:** While ten studies were replicated, it's important to consider whether the selected studies represent the full spectrum of human-assistant interaction research.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper is significant as it provides a viable alternative or complement to human-in-the-loop studies in the context of human-assistant interaction design, specifically making it cheaper and more accessible to perform such studies. The integration of LLMs is a promising tool to reduce labor costs and the ethical issues linked to privacy and human subject management. Overall, it represents a significant step forward in the methodology of this field.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7.5**.

*   **Reasoning for the score:** The paper presents a significant contribution to human-assistant interaction research by demonstrating a feasible and scalable approach to simulating human behavior. While there are limitations regarding simulation fidelity and reliance on GPT-4, the platform's modular design, rigorous evaluation, and commitment to open source provide a solid foundation for future research. The work addresses a critical problem, introduces a novel solution, and validates it through comprehensive replication, suggesting a potentially high impact on the field. It still lacks some key features that could boost the novelty and significance to 9 or 10. For example, an in-depth comparison against other simulation methods or a more robust analysis of the LLM's inherent biases would significantly strengthen the paper.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Personalizing Large Language Models using Retrieval Augmented Generation and Knowledge Graph
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09945v1)
- **Authors**: Deeksha Prahlad, Chanhee Lee, Dongha Kim, Hokeun Kim
- **Abstract**: The advent of large language models (LLMs) has allowed numerous applications, including the generation of queried responses, to be leveraged in chatbots and other conversational assistants. Being trained on a plethora of data, LLMs often undergo high levels of over-fitting, resulting in the generation of extra and incorrect data, thus causing hallucinations in output generation. One of the root causes of such problems is the lack of timely, factual, and personalized information fed to the LLM. In this paper, we propose an approach to address these problems by introducing retrieval augmented generation (RAG) using knowledge graphs (KGs) to assist the LLM in personalized response generation tailored to the users. KGs have the advantage of storing continuously updated factual information in a structured way. While our KGs can be used for a variety of frequently updated personal data, such as calendar, contact, and location data, we focus on calendar data in this paper. Our experimental results show that our approach works significantly better in understanding personal information and generating accurate responses compared to the baseline LLMs using personal data as text inputs, with a moderate reduction in response time.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a method for personalizing large language models (LLMs) using Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) and knowledge graphs (KGs). The approach aims to address issues of hallucination (generating incorrect or nonsensical information) and lack of personalization in LLMs by leveraging KGs to store and retrieve updated, factual, and personalized information (specifically calendar data).  The method involves building a KG from user's calendar and conversational data, embedding the KG, and using a RAG pipeline to retrieve relevant information from the KG to augment the LLM's response generation. Experiments compare this approach with a baseline RAG system that directly uses text-based personal data. Results suggest the KG-augmented RAG approach performs better in terms of response accuracy (ROUGE and BLEU scores) and, surprisingly, has faster execution times.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The core idea of using KGs to enhance RAG for LLM personalization is not entirely new, but the paper makes a valuable contribution by focusing on calendar data and demonstrating improvements in the quality and speed of response generation. While others have explored KG-enhanced LLMs and RAG for personalization, this paper brings the focus on building KGs for efficiently capturing the dynamics in personal event data.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses a crucial issue of LLM applicability in personal assistant applications. The ability to personalize responses and reduce hallucination by integrating rapidly changing data (like schedules) is important for practical deployment. Furthermore, the demonstrated improvement in response time, even if modest, is significant given the latency constraints of interactive applications, especially considering it is meant to be deployed on personal devices.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the issues of hallucination and lack of personalization in LLMs, particularly for user-specific data.
*   **Well-Defined Approach:** The proposed method is clearly explained, with details of KG construction, embedding, and RAG pipeline.
*   **Experimental Results:** The results, showing improvements in ROUGE, BLEU, and response time compared to the baseline, are positive and support the claims.
*   **Focus on Efficiency:** The choice of smaller LLMs (Llama 2) and the focus on execution time are relevant for on-device deployment.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:**  The evaluation is limited to calendar data. The applicability of the approach to other types of personal information (e.g., contacts, emails) is not explored.
*   **KG Construction:** The paper provides only high-level detail on how KGs are automatically constructed from calendar and conversation data using SpaCy. There are other named entity and relation extraction approaches in the literature, and exploring the strengths and weaknesses of different KG extraction approaches would increase the rigor of the study.
*   **Dataset Generation Biases:** While generating a dataset via a LLM (ChatGPT-4) is an approach to bootstrap evaluations, the dataset inevitably embodies biases inherent in ChatGPT-4's training data and prompt design. These biases aren't critically addressed or characterized. If ChatGPT 4 is used for *both* the dataset *and* the golden answer, the evaluation may be significantly less rigorous.

**Overall Impression:**

The paper presents a promising approach to personalizing LLMs using RAG and KGs. While the scope is relatively limited, and the dataset generation may involve some biases, the demonstrated improvements in response accuracy and speed warrant further research. The clarity of the approach and positive experimental results make this a worthwhile contribution.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a practical and relevant approach, showcasing measurable improvements. However, the limited scope of calendar data, the lack of details on KG construction, and the limitations introduced by the reliance on a ChatGPT-generated dataset prevent it from achieving a higher score. Further investigations into the generalizability of the approach, a detailed discussion of different relation extraction methods, and a thorough exploration of dataset biases are necessary to significantly improve the paper's novelty and impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Pre-Act: Multi-Step Planning and Reasoning Improves Acting in LLM Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09970v1)
- **Authors**: Mrinal Rawat, Ambuje Gupta, Rushil Goomer, Alessandro Di Bari, Neha Gupta, Roberto Pieraccini
- **Abstract**: The ReAct (Reasoning + Action) capability in large language models (LLMs) has become the foundation of modern agentic systems. Recent LLMs, such as DeepSeek-R1 and OpenAI o1/o3, exemplify this by emphasizing reasoning through the generation of ample intermediate tokens, which help build a strong premise before producing the final output tokens. In this paper, we introduce Pre-Act, a novel approach that enhances the agent's performance by creating a multi-step execution plan along with the detailed reasoning for the given user input. This plan incrementally incorporates previous steps and tool outputs, refining itself after each step execution until the final response is obtained. Our approach is applicable to both conversational and non-conversational agents. To measure the performance of task-oriented agents comprehensively, we propose a two-level evaluation framework: (1) turn level and (2) end-to-end. Our turn-level evaluation, averaged across five models, shows that our approach, Pre-Act, outperforms ReAct by 70% in Action Recall on the Almita dataset. While this approach is effective for larger models, smaller models crucial for practical applications, where latency and cost are key constraints, often struggle with complex reasoning tasks required for agentic systems. To address this limitation, we fine-tune relatively small models such as Llama 3.1 (8B & 70B) using the proposed Pre-Act approach. Our experiments show that the fine-tuned 70B model outperforms GPT-4, achieving a 69.5% improvement in action accuracy (turn-level) and a 28% improvement in goal completion rate (end-to-end) on the Almita (out-of-domain) dataset.
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper "Pre-Act: Multi-Step Planning and Reasoning Improves Acting in LLM Agents":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Pre-Act, an enhanced version of the ReAct framework for LLM-based agents. Pre-Act improves agent performance by generating a multi-step execution plan with detailed reasoning for each action. This plan is refined iteratively as steps are executed and observations are made. The authors also propose a fine-tuning strategy leveraging datasets adapted for Pre-Act, enabling smaller models to achieve performance comparable to larger proprietary LLMs. Finally, they introduce a two-level evaluation framework (turn-level and end-to-end) to comprehensively assess agent performance. Experiments demonstrate that Pre-Act outperforms ReAct, particularly in action recall, and that fine-tuned smaller models can match or surpass the performance of much larger models like GPT-4.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of multi-step planning in LLM agents is not entirely novel, as the authors acknowledge. However, the specific approach of generating a comprehensive plan with detailed reasoning for each step, iteratively refining it based on observations, is a valuable contribution. Adapting datasets for Pre-Act training and proposing a two-level evaluation framework also enhances the work's novelty.

*   **Significance:** Improving the performance of LLM agents, especially making smaller models more effective, is highly significant. The reduction in latency and cost achieved through fine-tuning smaller models is critical for real-world deployment. The two-level evaluation framework provides a more comprehensive way to measure agent performance, considering both individual action accuracy and overall task success. The performance gains on the out-of-domain dataset demonstrate promising generalization capabilities.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clearly articulated problem and solution.
    *   Well-defined approach with Pre-Act, fine-tuning, and evaluation framework.
    *   Comprehensive experiments and results demonstrating the effectiveness of Pre-Act.
    *   Demonstration of the feasibility of using smaller fine-tuned models to achieve high performance.
    *   Introduction of a valuable two-level evaluation framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper acknowledges existing work on planning in LLMs, which slightly reduces the novelty. The approach of generating structured plans can also be computationally expensive. While the authors highlight reducing latency with fine-tuning, the generation of the initial multi-step plan may still be a bottleneck in some applications.
    *   The reliance on the proprietary dataset is also a limitation, as it makes the results less reproducible.
    *   The use of LLM-as-a-judge, while common, introduces subjectivity and potential biases in the evaluation. More deterministic evaluation metrics could further strengthen the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by promoting the use of multi-step planning in LLM agents and demonstrating the effectiveness of fine-tuning smaller models for agentic tasks. The proposed evaluation framework can also serve as a valuable tool for future research in this area.

*   **Score Rationale:**
    While the paper presents a valuable enhancement to the ReAct framework and demonstrates promising results, the core concept of planning is not entirely new. The fine-tuning approach is also well-established, although the specific application to Pre-Act and the achieved performance gains are significant. The limitations associated with the proprietary dataset and reliance on LLM-as-a-judge slightly reduce the overall impact. However, the performance improvements and comprehensive evaluation framework make the paper a solid contribution to the field.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Analysing Safety Risks in LLMs Fine-Tuned with Pseudo-Malicious Cyber Security Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09974v1)
- **Authors**: Adel ElZemity, Budi Arief, Shujun Li
- **Abstract**: The integration of large language models (LLMs) into cyber security applications presents significant opportunities, such as enhancing threat analysis and malware detection, but can also introduce critical risks and safety concerns, including personal data leakage and automated generation of new malware. We present a systematic evaluation of safety risks in fine-tuned LLMs for cyber security applications. Using the OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications framework, we assessed seven open-source LLMs: Phi 3 Mini 3.8B, Mistral 7B, Qwen 2.5 7B, Llama 3 8B, Llama 3.1 8B, Gemma 2 9B, and Llama 2 70B. Our evaluation shows that fine-tuning reduces safety resilience across all tested LLMs (e.g., the safety score of Llama 3.1 8B against prompt injection drops from 0.95 to 0.15). We propose and evaluate a safety alignment approach that carefully rewords instruction-response pairs to include explicit safety precautions and ethical considerations. This approach demonstrates that it is possible to maintain or even improve model safety while preserving technical utility, offering a practical path forward for developing safer fine-tuning methodologies. This work offers a systematic evaluation for safety risks in LLMs, enabling safer adoption of generative AI in sensitive domains, and contributing towards the development of secure, trustworthy, and ethically aligned LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the safety risks associated with fine-tuning Large Language Models (LLMs) using pseudo-malicious cyber security data. It evaluates the impact of fine-tuning on the resilience of seven open-source LLMs against various vulnerabilities defined by the OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications framework. The study finds that fine-tuning generally reduces safety resilience, making the LLMs more susceptible to attacks like prompt injection and sensitive information disclosure.  To mitigate this, the authors propose a novel safety alignment approach that involves carefully rewording instruction-response pairs to include explicit safety precautions and ethical considerations. They demonstrate that this approach can maintain or even improve model safety while preserving technical utility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its systematic approach to evaluating safety risks in fine-tuned LLMs specifically for cyber security applications. While previous work has explored safety issues in LLMs, the focus on the cyber security domain, the use of the OWASP framework in this context, and the introduction of a safety alignment technique are all novel contributions.  The use of a pseudo-malicious dataset is a practical compromise, although the inherent limitations of simulated data need to be acknowledged.  While aspects of the safety alignment approach might be inspired by existing alignment techniques, its specific adaptation to cyber security use cases offers a new angle.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its practical implications for the safe deployment of LLMs in security-sensitive applications. As LLMs become increasingly integrated into cyber security tools, understanding and mitigating potential vulnerabilities is crucial. The finding that fine-tuning can decrease safety resilience is a significant warning. The safety alignment approach provides a valuable direction for future research and development of safer fine-tuning methodologies.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Systematic Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough and systematic evaluation of safety risks using a well-defined framework (OWASP Top 10).
    *   **Practical Approach:**  The proposed safety alignment approach is practical and can be implemented without requiring significant modifications to existing fine-tuning pipelines.
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The study considers various LLM architectures and sizes, providing a comprehensive understanding of the impact of fine-tuning on safety resilience.
    *   **Validation:** The authors utilize two independent testing frameworks (DeepEval and Garak) strengthening the reliability of the results.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Pseudo-Malicious Data:** The use of pseudo-malicious data is a limitation, as it may not fully capture the complexities and nuances of real-world attacks.  How well do step-by-step descriptions and pseudo-code approximate the risks posed by real malicious code?  This must be considered.
    *   **Limited Scope of Alignment:** The safety alignment approach, while promising, has a limited scope. It focuses on rewording instruction-response pairs. More sophisticated alignment techniques might be necessary to address more complex safety concerns.
    *   **Generalizability:** The experiments are conducted on a specific set of LLMs and datasets. While the authors choose these LLMs to be representative, more work needs to be done to understand the generalizability of the findings to other models and domains.
    *   **Reliance on Evaluation Tools:**  The reliance on DeepEval and Garak introduces a dependency on the completeness and biases present within these tools.  While these tools are helpful, the assessment is only as good as their test prompts.
    *   **Inference Time Increase:** The notable increase in inference time post fine-tuning can be a significant constraint when choosing a model for many real-world applications.
    *   **Lack of detail in Safety Alignment methodology:** More detail in the safety alignment methodology to assist with replication and further work could be beneficial.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by systematically investigating safety risks in fine-tuned LLMs for cyber security applications. The introduction of a practical safety alignment approach is a positive step towards developing safer fine-tuning methodologies. The comprehensive analysis across different LLM architectures and sizes strengthens the paper's findings. However, the limitations associated with the use of pseudo-malicious data, the limited scope of alignment, and generalizability concerns temper the overall impact.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Ordered-subsets Multi-diffusion Model for Sparse-view CT Reconstruction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09985v1)
- **Authors**: Pengfei Yu, Bin Huang, Minghui Zhang, Weiwen Wu, Shaoyu Wang, Qiegen Liu
- **Abstract**: Score-based diffusion models have shown significant promise in the field of sparse-view CT reconstruction. However, the projection dataset is large and riddled with redundancy. Consequently, applying the diffusion model to unprocessed data results in lower learning effectiveness and higher learning difficulty, frequently leading to reconstructed images that lack fine details. To address these issues, we propose the ordered-subsets multi-diffusion model (OSMM) for sparse-view CT reconstruction. The OSMM innovatively divides the CT projection data into equal subsets and employs multi-subsets diffusion model (MSDM) to learn from each subset independently. This targeted learning approach reduces complexity and enhances the reconstruction of fine details. Furthermore, the integration of one-whole diffusion model (OWDM) with complete sinogram data acts as a global information constraint, which can reduce the possibility of generating erroneous or inconsistent sinogram information. Moreover, the OSMM's unsupervised learning framework provides strong robustness and generalizability, adapting seamlessly to varying sparsity levels of CT sinograms. This ensures consistent and reliable performance across different clinical scenarios. Experimental results demonstrate that OSMM outperforms traditional diffusion models in terms of image quality and noise resilience, offering a powerful and versatile solution for advanced CT imaging in sparse-view scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Ordered-subsets Multi-diffusion Model for Sparse-view CT Reconstruction":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces an ordered-subsets multi-diffusion model (OSMM) for sparse-view CT reconstruction.  The key idea is to divide the CT projection data into equal subsets and train multiple diffusion models (MSDM) on each subset independently.  This focuses learning on specific details within each subset. The overall approach also incorporates a single diffusion model trained on the entire sinogram (OWDM) to act as a global constraint, ensuring consistency and reducing the risk of errors. This unsupervised learning framework aims to improve robustness and generalizability across different sparsity levels and clinical scenarios. The authors demonstrate improved image quality and noise resilience compared to traditional diffusion models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The main novelty lies in the combination of ordered-subsets strategy (inspired by OSEM) with multi-diffusion models, specifically tailored to the projection domain for CT reconstruction.  While diffusion models are not entirely new to CT reconstruction, the approach of dividing projection data into subsets for *independent* learning and then using a global constraint *in the sinogram domain* seems novel. The introduction of *MSDM* and *OWDM* components and their combined effect is a significant advance in the current diffusion model approaches.

**Significance:** The problems tackled by this paper are undeniably important.  Sparse-view CT reconstruction is essential for reducing radiation dose. The OSMM approach promises to improve image quality and reduce artifacts, which can lead to better diagnostic outcomes. The unsupervised nature of the method is also significant as it reduces the need for large paired training datasets, which are difficult to obtain in medical imaging.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing diffusion models for sparse-view CT, specifically addressing issues of large datasets, redundancy, and difficulty capturing fine details.
*   **Sound Methodology:**  The proposed OSMM is well-motivated, and the architecture makes logical sense. Dividing the projection data for targeted learning is a sensible approach to reduce complexity. Integrating global information from the entire sinogram is also essential to maintain consistency.
*   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper includes experiments on multiple datasets, including simulated abdominal data (AAPM), a phantom dataset (CIRS), and preclinical mouse data, demonstrating both quantitative and qualitative improvements over other methods.  The ablation study effectively demonstrates the value of the MSDM and OWDM components.
*   **Unsupervised Nature:** The method works in an unsupervised manner, significantly reducing the requirement for large paired datasets.
*   **Reproducible Code:** The availability of code promotes reproducibility and further research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Complexity:** While the authors mention this briefly, the computational cost of training multiple diffusion models could be a significant barrier to wider adoption. The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of the computational demands.
*   **Subset Selection Strategy:** While ordered subsets is inspired by OSEM, further justification for the particular division strategy would be useful. Is round-robin optimal? Are other strategies explored? What is the effect of different values of N? These details are not provided.
*   **Limited Comparisons:** Although a variety of comparative methods are included, expanding the comparison to include very recent state-of-the-art diffusion models designed specifically for image generation could strengthen the paper.
*   **Parameter Sensitivity:** The paper could benefit from a discussion of the sensitivity of the model's performance to different hyperparameter settings. This would provide insights into the robustness and practical applicability of the method.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has good potential to influence the field of CT reconstruction. The combination of ordered subsets with diffusion models addresses a critical need for high-quality sparse-view reconstruction.  The unsupervised nature of the method is a significant advantage.  If the computational cost can be managed effectively, this approach could become a valuable tool for reducing radiation dose in CT imaging.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and well-motivated approach to sparse-view CT reconstruction with the potential to significantly improve image quality and reduce radiation dose. The core idea of combining ordered subsets with multi-diffusion models is innovative and is supported by comprehensive experimental results on multiple datasets. The unsupervised nature is also a significant strength. While the computational complexity needs further consideration, and some aspects of the methodology could be explored in greater depth, the overall contribution is substantial and warrants a high score.

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 8/10

### From Air to Wear: Personalized 3D Digital Fashion with AR/VR Immersive 3D Sketching
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09998v1)
- **Authors**: Ying Zang, Yuanqi Hu, Xinyu Chen, Yuxia Xu, Suhui Wang, Chunan Yu, Lanyun Zhu, Deyi Ji, Xin Xu, Tianrun Chen
- **Abstract**: In the era of immersive consumer electronics, such as AR/VR headsets and smart devices, people increasingly seek ways to express their identity through virtual fashion. However, existing 3D garment design tools remain inaccessible to everyday users due to steep technical barriers and limited data. In this work, we introduce a 3D sketch-driven 3D garment generation framework that empowers ordinary users - even those without design experience - to create high-quality digital clothing through simple 3D sketches in AR/VR environments. By combining a conditional diffusion model, a sketch encoder trained in a shared latent space, and an adaptive curriculum learning strategy, our system interprets imprecise, free-hand input and produces realistic, personalized garments. To address the scarcity of training data, we also introduce KO3DClothes, a new dataset of paired 3D garments and user-created sketches. Extensive experiments and user studies confirm that our method significantly outperforms existing baselines in both fidelity and usability, demonstrating its promise for democratized fashion design on next-generation consumer platforms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach for creating personalized 3D garments using AR/VR immersive sketching. It allows users, even those without design expertise, to create 3D clothing models by simply sketching in a 3D AR/VR environment.  The core of the method is a generative AI model that translates these 3D sketches into detailed, realistic garment models. The system combines a conditional diffusion model, a sketch encoder trained in a shared latent space, and an adaptive curriculum learning strategy to interpret imprecise, free-hand input. The authors also contribute a new dataset (KO3DClothes) of paired 3D garments and user-created sketches to address data scarcity.
